</span><span class="p">}</span><span class="w">
</span></code></pre></div></div></p>
<h3 id="verification_summary-how-to-verify">How to verify</h3>
<p>VSA consumers use VSAs to accomplish goals based on delegated trust. We call the
process of establishing a VSA’s authenticity and determining whether it meets
the consumer’s goals ‘verification’. Goals differ, as do levels of confidence
in VSA producers, so the verification procedure changes to suit its context.
However, there are certain steps that most verification procedures have in
common.</p>
<p>Verification MUST include the following steps:</p>
<ol>
<li>
<p>Verify the signature on the VSA envelope using the preconfigured roots of
trust. This step ensures that the VSA was produced by a trusted producer
and that it hasn’t been tampered with.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Verify the statement’s <code>subject</code> matches the digest of the artifact in
question. This step ensures that the VSA pertains to the intended artifact.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Verify that the <code>predicateType</code> is
<code>https://slsa.dev/verification_summary/v1</code>. This step ensures that the
in-toto predicate is using this version of the VSA format.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Verify that the <code>verifier</code> matches the public key (or equivalent) used to
verify the signature in step 1. This step identifies the VSA producer in
cases where their identity is not implicitly revealed in step 1.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Verify that the value for <code>resourceUri</code> in the VSA matches the expected
value. This step ensures that the consumer is using the VSA for the
producer’s intended purpose.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Verify that the value for <code>verificationResult</code> is <code>PASSED</code>. This step
ensures the artifact is suitable for the consumer’s purposes.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Verify that <code>verifiedLevels</code> contains the expected value. This step ensures
that the artifact is suitable for the consumer’s purposes.</p>
</li>
</ol>
<p>Verification MAY additionally contain the following step:</p>
<ol>
<li>(Optional) Verify additional fields required to determine whether the VSA
meets your goal.</li>
</ol>
<p>Verification mitigates different threats depending on the VSA’s contents and the
verification procedure.</p>
<p>IMPORTANT: A VSA does not protect against compromise of the verifier, such as by
a malicious insider. Instead, VSA consumers SHOULD carefully consider which
verifiers they add to their roots of trust.</p>
<h4 id="verification_summary-examples">Examples</h4>
<ol>
<li>
<p>Suppose consumer C wants to delegate to verifier V the decision for whether
to accept artifact A as resource R. Consumer C verifies that:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<p>The signature on the VSA envelope using V’s public signing key from their
preconfigured root of trust.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>subject</code> is A.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>predicateType</code> is <code>https://slsa.dev/verification_summary/v1</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>verifier.id</code> is V.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>resourceUri</code> is R.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>slsaResult</code> is <code>PASSED</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>verifiedLevels</code> contains <code>SLSA_BUILD_LEVEL_UNEVALUATED</code>.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<p>Note: This example is analogous to traditional code signing. The expected
value for <code>verifiedLevels</code> is arbitrary but prenegotiated by the producer and
the consumer. The consumer does not need to check additional fields, as C
fully delegates the decision to V.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Suppose consumer C wants to enforce the rule “Artifact A at resource R must
have a passing VSA from verifier V showing it meets SLSA Build Level 2+.”
Consumer C verifies that:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<p>The signature on the VSA envelope using V’s public signing key from their
preconfigured root of trust.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>subject</code> is A.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>predicateType</code> is <code>https://slsa.dev/verification_summary/v1</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>verifier.id</code> is V.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>resourceUri</code> is R.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>slsaResult</code> is <code>PASSED</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><code>verifiedLevels</code> is <code>SLSA_BUILD_LEVEL_2</code> or <code>SLSA_BUILD_LEVEL_3</code>.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<p>Note: In this example, verifying the VSA mitigates the same threats as
verifying the artifact’s SLSA provenance. See
<a href="/spec/v1.0/verifying-artifacts">Verifying artifacts</a> for details about which
threats are addressed by verifying each SLSA level.</p>
</li>
</ol>
<div id="slsaresult">
<h3 id="verification_summary-emslsaresult-stringem"><em>SlsaResult (String)</em></h3>
</div>
<p>The result of evaluating an artifact (or set of artifacts) against SLSA.
SHOULD be</p>
<ul>
<li>The <a href="#verification_summary-tracks">SLSA Track</a> level the referenced artifact qualifies for as
<code>SLSA_&lt;TRACK_NAME&gt;_LEVEL_&lt;LEVEL_NUMBER&gt;</code>, or</li>
<li><code>SLSA_&lt;TRACK NAME&gt;_LEVEL_UNEVALUATED</code> if the VSA issuer does not want to
make a claim about the track level an artifact meets</li>
</ul>
<p>For example:</p>
<ul>
<li><code>SLSA_BUILD_LEVEL_UNEVALUATED</code></li>
<li><code>SLSA_BUILD_LEVEL_0</code></li>
<li><code>SLSA_BUILD_LEVEL_3</code></li>
<li><code>SLSA_SOURCE_LEVEL_2</code></li>
<li><code>SLSA_SOURCE_LEVEL_4</code></li>
<li><code>FAILED</code> (Indicates policy evaluation failed)</li>
</ul>
<p>Note that each SLSA level implies the levels below it in the same track.
For example, <code>SLSA_BUILD_LEVEL_3</code> means (<code>SLSA_BUILD_LEVEL_1</code> +
<code>SLSA_BUILD_LEVEL_2</code> + <code>SLSA_BUILD_LEVEL_3</code>).</p>
<p>Users MAY use custom values here but MUST NOT use custom values starting with
<code>SLSA_</code>.</p>
<h3 id="verification_summary-change-history">Change history</h3>
<ul>
<li>1.2:
<ul>
<li>Update SlsaResult definition to discuss how to refer to new tracks and
link to <a href="#verified-properties">verified properties</a> for additional SLSA
endorsed values.</li>
<li>Fixed a typo where the verificationResult was incorrectly referred to
as slsaResult.</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>1.1:
<ul>
<li>Changed the <code>policy</code> object to recommend that the <code>digest</code> field of
the <code>ResourceDescriptor</code> is set.</li>
<li>Added optional <code>verifier.version</code> field to record verification tools.</li>
<li>Added Verification subsection with examples.</li>
<li>Made <code>timeVerified</code> optional.</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>1.0:
<ul>
<li>Replaced <code>materials</code> with <code>resolvedDependencies</code>.</li>
<li>Relaxed <code>SlsaResult</code> to allow other values.</li>
<li>Converted to lowerCamelCase for consistency with <a href="/provenance">SLSA Provenance</a>.</li>
<li>Added <code>slsaVersion</code> field.</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>0.2:
<ul>
<li>Added <code>resource_uri</code> field.</li>
<li>Added optional <code>input_attestations</code> field.</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>0.1: Initial version.</li>
</ul>
   <!-- /spec/v1.2/verification_summary ends -->
